#7290. Head of Talent at VRChat

Your mandate is to help VRChat build a small, exceptionally strong team: translate business needs into clear hiring criteria, proactively find outstanding people, and drive searches from initial scoping through close.

This role suits someone who has built a hiring function from the ground up before, who thinks like a strategic partner to leadership but is equally comfortable being the one sourcing, screening, and closing candidates day to day.

● Demonstrated ability to operate as both a strategic advisor to leadership and a hands-on individual contributor

● Strong technical sourcing background, with a track record of hiring for engineering or other hard-to-fill technical roles

● Experience building recruiting systems and effective onboarding handoffs

● Track record of strong hiring outcomes, measurable improvements in time-to-hire, offer acceptance rate, or similar

● Comfortable advising senior leadership on org design and headcount strategy, including pushing back thoughtfully when needed

● Excellent communication and stakeholder management skills across all levels of an organization

● Experience recruiting for technical, consumer, social, or platform-based products

● Experience in lean environments that required flexibility beyond recruiting, such as workforce planning, manager enablement, or building foundational People practices ● Exposure to hiring in areas like trust & safety, content moderation, or community-focused roles

● Demonstrated interest in VR, online communities, or being a VRChat user and creator

● Familiarity with how VRChat's platform and community work

Work from anywhere! VRChat is a 100% remote company offering flexible working hours in combination with core hours supporting real-time collaboration

401K for US & Group RRSP for Canadian Employees

VRChat fosters a diverse, creative, and collaborative environment where anyone can contribute to any of the ongoing projects or direction of the roadmap at any time.  If you’re a passionate team player who wants to have an impact on a dynamic team, we’d love to hear from you!

All job offers are subject to early stage ID checks, satisfactory referencing and background checks.

#7288. X launches updated Lead Gen Ads globally

The company’s refreshed product features a prominent call-to-action button and is designed to turn engagement into leads.

After launching its revamped Lead Generation Ads product in August, X has now announced that all advertisers globally are able to run Lead Gen Ad campaigns in the app.

As shown in this image, X’s refreshed Lead Gen Ads feature a prominent call-to-action button along the bottom of a promoted post, which is designed to drive a direct response.

The color of the CTA button will be dictated by ad visuals, making the format stand out even more in-stream.

That could make for more compelling promotions, which also enable easier transfer from interest to information gathering in the app.

As X explained in an email sent to business users: “You can now capture high-intent leads directly from the conversation, with less friction and fewer drop-offs. Whether you’re booking demos, growing email lists, capturing quote requests, filling webinars, or running sweepstakes, Lead Gen Ads turn real-time engagement on X into qualified leads you can act on immediately.”

Long-time social media marketers will recognize the format, with X essentially replicating the old Lead Gen Ads design that Twitter used to offer.

It’s back now, in X form, with the X team seemingly leaning into what’s worked in the past as a means to facilitate more engagement.

But then again, Twitter did retire the format in 2016 due to lack of advertiser interest.

Either way, X said that all elements of the updated Lead Gen Ads will be managed inside its Ads Manager, where marketers will be able to preview ads before they launch.

Advertisers will also be able to export leads via direct download, or directly link them to their chosen CRM. Various integrations are available to streamline the process.

#7287. Businesses Treat Your AI Agent 3 Different Ways via @sejournal, @slobodanmanic

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

An AI agent does things its customer would not, on that customer’s behalf. Three businesses answered that problem three ways in October.

People are starting to send AI agents to do things for them online: Buy the groceries, book the flight, check the bank balance. The agent goes to the business’s website and acts in the customer’s place. Every business now has to decide what to do when one shows up, and in October three big ones answered three different ways.

I think Walmart got it right: It is about to give each agent a separate profile inside the customer’s account. An AI agent will do things the person maybe wouldn’t have, so the two are not the same. But it does those things on the person’s behalf, so they need to be linked. A separate profile lets the business see both: the agent and the customer who sent it.

Walmart says why it built Agent Profiles: “Today, when a customer uses an AI agent, it signs in using a customer’s account. This means that all purchases – whether made by the customer or the agent – appear together, without any differentiation.”

An agent might browse more pages than the customer would, maybe all of them. That’s the most basic difference. Beyond that, it could be literally anything.

So it’s not as simple as letting the agent sign in as the customer. Send a dog to shop for you at a local market, with a set amount of money. The dog only does it to bring the shopping back to you, and the whole thing only makes sense if the sellers know who sent the dog.

Walmart’s spending limit is the set amount of money, and the profile is the sellers knowing who sent it. A block stops the customer from using an agent at all. A shared login hides the agent behind the customer.

DoorDash says outside AI agents have placed “maybe several hundred orders in total”, in CEO Tony Xu’s words on the Q3 call, as TechCrunch reported. In the same quarter DoorDash handled 993 million orders, more than 10 million a day.

The rules for how an agent tells a business who it is are still a draft. The AI customer-service company Sierra and Meta are writing them as the Personal Agent Protocol: 40-plus companies are helping shape it, and its website named no business using it with customers when I checked on 1 November. On October 30, the draft added a recommendation that agents sign their requests with Web Bot Auth, a proposed way for an agent to prove which company runs it. According to the bot-protection company DataDome, as PPC Land reported, Meta’s Muse agent has been signing some of its requests that way since around 12 October, without an announcement.

Do three things before agents arrive in numbers:

If you can’t tell the agent from the customer, you can’t treat it differently.

This post was originally published on No Hacks.

#7285. Reddit pitches its 1,300 gaming-related communities to brands

The company said that in-app conversations “created and shaped by real players” can drive engagement and influence purchasing decisions.

Reddit shared new data on its evolving in-app gaming conversation with a playbook for advertisers that are seeking to get their promotions in front of gamers and gaming-related audiences.

Based on research conducted by TransUnion, Reddit said that gaming marketers are largely failing to capitalize on the opportunities afforded by its highly engaged communities.

As explained by Reddit: “Reddit is home to more than 1,300 gaming-related communities, where conversations are created and shaped by real players. These communities don't just bring gamers together; they influence discovery, engagement, and even purchasing decisions.”

Underlining this, Reddit shared additional notes on gaming engagement, and what that means for marketing approaches.

First, according to comparative analysis of Reddit promotions, Reddit ads drove an average of $8.58 return-on-ad-spend for gaming advertisers.

The analysis also found that Reddit discussions and engagement led to broader halo effect across other channels, boosting overall interest in the advertised products.

As explained by Reddit: “This means Reddit’s modeled contribution included value beyond its own platform, demonstrating how Reddit can complement other media touchpoints and contribute to broader marketing performance. TV accounted for 18% of Reddit’s total synergy, followed by paid search at 17%, online video at 15%, and paid social at 12%.”

In other words, Reddit’s gaming communities are influential, which can expand the value of in-app promotions.

On the basis of the last point, the analysis suggested that gaming brands have significantly underinvested in Reddit ads, with Reddit representing just 5% of paid social investment, on average.

Of course, that’s assuming that these same trends would hold true if more gaming ads were present in the app. But based on the data included in the analysis, there might be more opportunity and potential for gaming marketers there.

The report also said that brands that ran a full-funnel strategy across key campaign objectives saw 19% stronger overall ROAS than the single-tactic average. The report added that video views delivered the strongest ROAS.

There are some interesting data points here, though the parameters of the study make it impossible to establish a true like-for-like comparison in relative ad spend.

But the indications suggest that Reddit ads could deliver value and unique audience reach for gaming marketers.

#7282. Reddit Begins Testing Ads In Its Own Search Results via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Ads are being tested in the community platform's own search results with a select group of advertisers, matched partly by relevance to what people search.

Reddit is testing ads in its own search results pages with a select group of eligible advertisers.

The company announced the test in a short newsroom post on Tuesday and said it will evaluate how users respond so it can refine the experience over time. The post doesn’t say how advertisers were chosen or whether others can ask to join.

“These placements leverage existing ad targeting in search results pages on Reddit, which have high purchase and search intent.”

“Ads in search may appear based on a range of factors, including contextual relevance to a user’s search query.”

The post does not mention what else would factor into which ads appear. In the example, a person searching for “espresso station” might see ads for coffee beans, mugs, or espresso machines, and “home office setup” may lead to advertisements for desks, office chairs, or monitors.

Example screens in the post show the mobile version of the Reddit application after searching for “home office setup”. Each screen shows a card labeled “Ad” containing a picture of a product (with a website address) and a “Shop Now” button. One example shows a card sitting under a blurb citing subreddits as its sources. Another example shows a card placed between two posts.

Brooke Osmundson reported that in October 2024, Reddit extended the keyword targeting it uses in its conversation placements to the Feed of the platform.

In its first quarter shareholder letter, Reddit stated that the product placements allow users searching on the site to browse listings and shop right from within those search results. In a phone call with investors after their Q1 earnings were released (April 30), Chief Operating Officer Jennifer Wong stated that Reddit didn’t “even have ads in certain high growing surfaces like search, for example.” On this same call, CEO Steve Huffman reported that “Search WAUqs are up 30% year-over-year.” Search WAUqs is Reddit’s shorthand for weekly active uniques who search.

During a Q2 investor call, Wong referred to search as “a space that we’re eyeing” for advertising and mentioned that Reddit has the infrastructure “already queued up,” according to Roger Montti. Wong also referenced Shopping Listing Ads, a format that groups several retailers’ products in one module, as examples of the type of advertisements she believes could be used on a search page.

Beyond eligibility, Reddit’s post is also silent regarding the other key elements of how the test works, such as markets, ad format types, ad pricing models, and whether it covers more than the app used in its examples.

Reddit is pitching its search pages to advertisers on the strength of what people type there. For now, Reddit’s search ads are limited to the advertisers it selected for the test. You can ask your Reddit rep whether your account is part of the test and how search impressions would show up in reporting.

Advertisers outside the test can watch for search to appear among Reddit’s ad placements. The post gives no end date for the test and no timeline for adding advertisers.

Follow on YouTube. Matt G. Southern is the Senior News Writer at Search Engine Journal, where he’s covered Google, SEO, ...

#7279. Meta ends support for Quest 2 and Quest Pro

Meta is shifting focus to its next-gen VR headset, which will be launched next year.

Meta announced that it will end feature development for its Quest 2 and Quest Pro VR devices by March 2027 as the company looks to the next stage of virtual reality development. The announcement comes ahead of the launch of its Meta VR glasses, which are set to launch next spring.

Meta’s Quest 2 device was launched in 2022, which was shortly after the company announced a broad-ranging metaverse vision that was centered around VR connection.

The Quest Pro was the evolution of this and included more advanced technology to enhance the VR experience.

Both devices, however, became less of a priority for the company over time as Meta shifted its attention to generative artificial intelligence. As a result, Quest headsets became something of an afterthought, with Meta now putting an end date on its support of its once-lauded device.

Meta said that feature development for both devices will end with v213, which will reach all devices in March.

“New OS features, platform capabilities, and APIs released after v213 will only be available on Quest 3, Quest 3S, and newer devices,” Meta said. “After v213, we’ll keep shipping security patches and critical bug fixes through the end of 2027.”

Meta noted that apps that support Quest 2 and Quest Pro will still be submittable through December 2027, which will be the final cut-off date for Quest 2 support.

That will leave Quest 3, which Meta launched in 2023, as Meta’s final supported VR device, at least until the company launches its next-level VR glasses next year.

Meta recently previewed the coming VR device, which impressed analysts at its Connect 2026 event.

The new device is lighter and more comfortable to use than previous iterations, and it also includes more advanced tech elements to power all-new VR experiences.

But it’ll be costly. Meta said that the device will cost $1299 at launch, which is more than double the current price of a Quest 3.

Given the general lack of consumer interest and engagement with VR, that seems like a big ask. Then again, if Meta invests more into VR development and the integration of its creative AI tools into the VR environment, that could spark a new wave of interest in the tech.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Google is expanding UCP-powered direct checkout in AI Mode and the Gemini app to Canadian shoppers, with a Merchant Center integration planned for next year.

Google is expanding direct checkout powered by the Universal Commerce Protocol (UCP) to shoppers in Canada across AI Mode in Google Search and the Gemini app.

Sabrina Geremia, Vice President and Country Managing Director at Google Canada, announced the rollout on November 4, 2026.

The feature allows Canadian shoppers to purchase directly from eligible retailers while researching products on Google’s AI surfaces.

Google also announced plans to bring simplified UCP integration directly into Merchant Center starting next year.

Here’s what’s changing with the Canadian rollout, how the checkout flow works, and what retailers managing Merchant Center feeds should prepare for.

According to Google, the initial Canadian launch includes four partners and merchant groups:

These partnerships allow Canadian shoppers to complete purchases inside AI Mode in Google Search and the Gemini app without leaving the conversation.

During checkout, shoppers pay with Google Pay using the payment methods and shipping details saved in Google Wallet.

Google says retailers remain the seller of record and can customize the integration to fit their business needs.

In the coming months, Google plans to work with retailers to add more capabilities to the checkout experience, such as applying loyalty rewards.

For retailers outside the initial launch group, broader access is tied to an upcoming Merchant Center update.

Starting next year, Google says it will make simplified UCP integration available directly inside Merchant Center.

This update will allow retailers to enable direct checkout and other capabilities for their product listings across AI Mode in Google Search and the Gemini app directly from their accounts.

Google launched UCP earlier this year alongside industry partners as an open standard for agentic commerce.

The protocol creates a shared language so AI agents, consumer surfaces, businesses, and payment providers can work together across product discovery, purchasing, and post-purchase support without building custom connections for every platform.

In the announcement, Mani Fazeli, VP of Product at Shopify, stated:

Canadians are already asking AI what to buy, and now Shopify merchants selling into Canada can be faithfully represented and bought from directly in AI Mode and the Gemini app, with zero extra work. UCP handles the complexity of agentic commerce underneath, so shoppers can buy what they find without leaving the conversation.

While Fazeli notes that Shopify merchants selling into Canada can be bought from directly with zero extra work, Google specifies that the initial rollout applies to select Shopify merchants.

Direct checkout in AI Mode and the Gemini app keeps more of the transaction inside Google’s interfaces while keeping the merchant as the seller of record.

Even before the Merchant Center UCP integration arrives next year, product feed quality remains the foundation for visibility across Google’s shopping and AI surfaces.

Google’s generative AI optimization guide states that generative AI responses can include product listings and product information where appropriate. Using Merchant Center feeds can help products appear in both AI responses and standard Google Search results.

For ecommerce teams and agencies managing Canadian accounts, here’s a few suggestions to help you stay ahead of what’s coming:

Keeping your product feeds clean now will make it easier to adopt the Merchant Center UCP settings once they become available.

Google hasn’t yet shared a specific release date next year for the simplified UCP integration in Merchant Center, or disclosed eligibility criteria and onboarding steps for Canadian retailers outside the initial launch group. It also remains unclear how UCP direct checkout orders are reported or attributed in Merchant Center and Google Ads, or how direct checkout interacts with paid Shopping and Performance Max campaigns.

We’ll update this article as Google shares additional details about Merchant Center rollout timing and broader availability.

#7270. Reddit tests ads in search results

The platform wants to help advertisers align their promotions with users’ discovery behavior even as the company’s overall search traffic drops.

Reddit is testing ads in search results in the app, as the platform looks for more ways to help advertisers align with discovery behaviors.

As shown in these example screens, Reddit’s search ads will be targeted by query, and will include a small ad marker in the upper section of the panel.

As explained by Reddit: “People come to Reddit seeking human perspectives and experiences they can’t find elsewhere, with half of Reddit users saying the platform helps them discover something new. As we continue improving the user search experience on Reddit, we’re also beginning to test ad placements in search. These placements leverage existing ad targeting in search results pages on Reddit, which have high purchase and search intent.”

Reddit said the promotions will be shown in search results based on a range of factors, though these will primarily be aligned with contextual relevance.

Reddit has been working to improve its discovery tools as the app tries to help users find more relevant matches for their queries.

In July, Reddit published a study that found that 72% of its users come to the platform seeking information, and 64% of them have a specific goal in mind.

That also aligns with how Reddit gains traffic from search results, since the platform gets significant citation volume from AI chatbots.

However, AI traffic has started to dry up. Last week, as part of its Q3 performance update, Reddit CEO Steve Huffman said that the company’s search traffic dropped by more than 1 million referrals per week on average in October.

That could reduce the value of its own in-app discovery process, though Tuesday’s announcement suggests that Reddit is clearly hoping to maintain its value as a product discovery tool.

Reddit said that it will initially test ads in search with a select group of eligible advertisers.

#7269. AI Visibility vs. Search Rankings: Is Your Agency Measuring the Wrong Thing For 2027?

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Rankings still matter, but AI reporting is built on a dashboard made for a different game. A three-gate model for measuring AI visibility that ties to intent.

This post was sponsored by WebPros. The opinions expressed in this article are the sponsor’s own.

Your client opens the monthly report. Rankings are up and the target terms sit on page one. Organic traffic is holding steady. Then the question lands: “So why are fewer good prospects reaching us?”

The instinct is to defend the rankings. The better answer is to admit the report measures the wrong thing, or more precisely, the right thing about a game that is no longer the only one in play.

Three questions are missing from that report. Is the client included when AI tools answer relevant questions? Is the business described accurately? And when a buyer asks for a recommendation, is the client actually the pick?

A ranking is a position in a list. The user still has to scan the options and choose one.

An AI answer isn’t a list, it’s a verdict. The model has already scanned and compared, and by the time the user reads the response, the shortlisting may already be done. You did not necessarily lose the click. You may never have been in the running to receive one.

The click did not just get harder to win. For a growing share of queries, it stopped being an available prize.

A citation and a recommendation are different things. A model can name you as one of five options while steadily recommending a competitor, or cite you to describe the category and then point elsewhere for the solution.

A mention is different again. You can appear in the answer without earning a citation, and you can earn a citation without being presented as a suitable option.

Mention frequency, reported on its own, is a vanity number wearing new-technology clothes. Agencies that spent the 2010s learning not to celebrate raw ranking positions are about to relearn the lesson with citations.

The answer is not to drop citation share. It is to stop treating it as a finish line and start treating it as the first of three gates.

The useful way to read an AI answer is through three gates. Each tells you something different, and each needs its own measurement.

Brand mentions and citation sources live here. A model that never surfaces you makes everything downstream moot. Inclusion buys a seat in the conversation and nothing more.

Check: Take a defined set of questions relevant to the client’s business and run them across the AI platforms being monitored.

Record: Track brand mentions and website citations separately, each as a share of all tested responses. If the client appears in 12 of 40 responses, the observed mention rate is 30%. That tells you something about the tested questions, not about every AI search in the market.

Act: Review the assets the model cites, whether documentation, service pages, or third-party reviews, to identify what to investigate and potentially reinforce.

But do not mistake a citation for an endorsement. The model may use your client’s article to explain an industry problem and recommend someone else to solve it.

Once you are in, does the model describe you correctly?

This is brand understanding, and it is the most underrated of the three. The model decides more than whether to name you. It decides what to say you are.

A technically accurate citation that frames you as a budget option or yesterday’s incumbent can cost more than being left out.

Consider a B2B software company that has expanded into enterprise customers but still has older product pages describing it as a tool for freelancers. An AI answer may repeat that positioning when an enterprise buyer asks for recommendations. The company appears in the conversation but loses relevance before the buyer ever reaches its website.

The source of that framing error may be an outdated product page, an old comparison article, or conflicting descriptions across the web. The point is to find it, not simply count the mention as a win.

Check: Compare AI-generated business descriptions with verified product facts.

Record: Save each exact description and flag what is outdated, missing, or incorrect. Calculate framing accuracy as the share of evaluated brand descriptions that are accurate, not as a share of all tested answers.

Act: Trace the likely source where possible, then correct outdated product pages or conflicting descriptions that the client can change.

This gate measures whether the client is explicitly recommended on decision-stage questions, alongside the competitors recommended in those answers. It is the gate closest to the buyer’s choice, not proof of a sale.

A brand can clear inclusion and framing and still lose here on the questions that decide a sale. This is the gate worth fighting over.

The distinction is easiest to see when you compare answers side by side.

Ask an AI tool to recommend software for a 10-person marketing agency. It mentions the client’s product, describes its features accurately, then recommends a competitor because it considers that competitor better suited to small teams.

Inclusion passed. Framing passed. Endorsement failed.

That tells the agency more than an increase in citation share ever could.

Check: Use questions that reflect actual buying decisions, not just informational searches.

Record: Note whether the client is explicitly recommended, which competitors are recommended, and the reasons given. Report the client’s recommendations as a share of all tested decision-stage answers, with the same calculation for each rival. More than one brand can be recommended in an answer.

Act: If the competitor wins because its positioning is clearer, investigate the client’s positioning. If the recommendation rests on an outdated feature comparison, identify and correct the information where possible.

Organic visibility and long-term trend lines do not vanish in this model. They run alongside it as the traditional baseline.

The three gates are distinct measures, not a conversion funnel. A rising citation count alongside falling endorsement share can signal trouble rather than progress.

There is a catch. AI answers are not fixed search results.

Ask the same question on different days, in different markets, or through different tools, and you may get different recommendations. Sometimes even repeated runs produce different answers.

That makes measurement discipline more important, not less.

An agency cannot claim that visibility improved because a brand appeared in eight answers this month and five last month if the underlying questions changed.

Keep the prompt set, market, language, AI platform, measurement dates, and number of repeated runs documented. And always show the denominator.

Eight mentions across 20 responses is not the same as eight mentions across 100. Neither number means much without knowing the questions being asked.

The same applies to framing and endorsement. An improvement in recommendation share matters only when the comparison is built on a consistent set of relevant, decision-stage questions.

The goal is not a perfect universal AI visibility score. It is a consistent measurement that shows what changed and gives the agency something useful to act on.

Keep three business signals separate when you compare them with AI endorsement trends:

Branded search also rises with PR, paid campaigns, and seasonality, so read it as a trend to compare with endorsement share, not as simple attribution.

Research on subsequent website visits provides context, not proof that endorsement increases branded search. A Similarweb study on what happens after AI recommends a brand found that users who saw a brand recommended by ChatGPT were more likely to visit that brand than its competitor within seven days.

When ChatGPT recommended American Express, 7.2% of users subsequently visited its website, compared with 3.1% who visited Capital One. When Capital One received the recommendation, 14.2% visited its website, compared with 3.8% who visited American Express.

The study used an opted-in US desktop panel from July–December 2025 and selected consumer brands, so the findings may not hold for smaller or B2B businesses. It is a useful signal, not a universal benchmark.

AI referral traffic is another signal. ChatGPT, Perplexity, and Gemini do send visits, and GA4 can show identifiable referrals. In Ahrefs’ opt-in Web Analytics panel, AI assistants accounted for less than 1% of site traffic in September 2026. That measures traffic received by the participating sites, not the percentage of AI-tool usage that sends an outbound click.

Track it, but do not expect it to tell the whole story.

A buyer who sees a recommendation in an AI answer may search for the brand on Google afterward. That visit appears in search reporting, not necessarily as an AI referral.

If endorsement share rises, branded search follows, and qualified leads increase, there is a useful story to investigate. There is not yet proof that the first caused the other two.

That distinction is worth making explicitly. Clients do not need inflated attribution claims. They need an informed view of where demand may be coming from and whether their visibility is improving on the questions that matter.

A useful report needs three things: the observation, the interpretation, and the next action.

Sample client report: Illustrative figures, not actual customer results

The example assumes comparable question sets and measurement conditions across both periods.

Notice what the report does not say. It does not claim that the increase in mentions generated the additional branded searches. It does not describe citation growth as a commercial win without checking endorsement.

Riva approaches the problem from a related but different angle. It evaluates whether AI systems can access a business’s website, understand its business and individual offerings, and surface those offerings against competitors.

Its measurement includes technical accessibility, information completeness, and offering-level visibility. The AI Visibility Score measures an offering’s visibility relative to competitors in the tested queries. Average Position indicates how prominently an offering appears when mentioned, while Query Success captures how many relevant queries produce an appearance.

Those are not three separate scores for inclusion, framing, and endorsement. The three gates are a framework for interpreting AI answers; Riva’s access, understanding, and visibility analysis helps explain where visibility problems may originate and what to improve.

Riva brings improvement recommendations into one place, giving agencies a way to move from observing a visibility problem to investigating what might be causing it.

Disclosure: I work for WebPros, the company behind Riva.

Want to see what AI tools understand about your business, where competitors appear instead, and what needs fixing? Explore Riva to assess your AI visibility and identify your next steps.

#7267. SERP Data Providers Say Google Is Throttling Goto Result Links via @sejournal, @MattGSouthern

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

SERP data providers say Google throttles goto links per results page, so some rank data may store Google redirect links in place of page URLs.

Google is restricting the speed at which anyone can follow (google.com/goto) redirects for search results on each page, as per DataForSEO and Serper. Both sell Google search result API access. Due to this restriction rank data provided by these services may contain Google redirect URLs instead of actual URLs.

According to DataForSEO, the restrictions mean that they have seen some of their request modes only resolving part of the destination URLs. Serper’s status page first noted the issue at 1:30 a.m. UTC on October 30. SerpApi, another seller of search data, also logged goto links for organic results on the same date. Neither DataForSEO nor Serper links to a Google announcement regarding the restriction and the statements below are from their websites.

DataForSEO stated in its November 2nd article that when you open multiple links from the same results page in rapid succession, Google will begin to throttle the redirect(s). Each link carries an identifier based on the results page it originated from.

When Google throttles your redirects, instead of sending your requests for resolution directly to the destination, Google will send back a page with a “Continue” button. That button will only become available after a waiting period. The amount of time the continue button is disabled grows the faster the previous links were resolved.

For example, take a results page that contains 18 goto links to other websites. If all 18 links are requested in quick succession, Google may allow the first 5-6 to be resolved immediately. However, the 7th link may result in a 1 second wait before being able to access the website. The 8th link may then take 3 seconds. And this process continues until the 15th link, where there could be a 40 second wait before accessing that website. DataForSEO reported that they had instances of waiting up to 120 seconds for 1 URL.

The limit is based on the results page and not based on the IP address or the search query used. Also, DataForSEO reports that simply switching IP addresses or search queries will not help bypass this limitation. Additionally, it says that even if you wait an hour or longer before resolving all of the links, Google still uses the same results page limits.

DataForSEO’s Standard Priority mode now resolves all goto URLs, although the added delays may mean execution takes longer. In High Priority and Live modes, the company says about half of goto URLs are resolved.

Serper’s status page says Google “limits how many /goto result links can be followed per results page.” Its first fix resolved the top three organic links and returned results four through 10 as goto links, which Serper said work about 30 seconds after the search. It added a /resolve endpoint to fetch the rest.

On Oct. 31, Serper said it had found another way to get the real URLs and that all result links had them again. It called that “likely temporary.”

SerpApi’s Status Page displays an October 30th event indicating that its Google Search API returned goto links instead of regular links for organic search results; it states that they had the issue fixed by November 2nd. However, there is a second incident reported on November 3rd reporting intermittent issues with the same problem. Neither incident gives a cause.

DataForSEO dates Google’s move to www.google.com/goto links in Search Results to late August. It says the links cannot be decoded, and therefore a tool will have to follow each link to find out where it goes. The link-following part is what the new limit slows down.

Some rank tracking services as well as in house reports using landing pages for URL-level reporting may display Google goto redirects (google.com/goto) in place of true destination URLs from approximately October 30th onward based upon the service provider and how the requests were made. If a specific URL is missing from a URL-level report it could still be showing up in a ranking position and have an unresolved redirect at the URL location. Prior to reporting a URL-level loss check whether the stored URL is a Google redirect, also contact your service provider and determine what method was used to collect the data.

As of my check on Nov. 4, SerpApi’s Incident Report on goto links from Nov. 3 is still listed as Identified. Serper states that in the event that Google also puts a cap on the method it uses for retrieving actual URLs, it would revert to automatically resolving the top three results and use /resolve to fetch the remaining after approximately thirty seconds. DataForSEO stated it will continue to adjust its processing based on what it observes regarding how Google deals with goto resolutions.

Reports covering the last few days of October through early November should be reviewed again at some point when providers have determined how they will handle goto resolution.

Follow on YouTube. Matt G. Southern is the Senior News Writer at Search Engine Journal, where he’s covered Google, SEO, ...

#7262. Instagram tries to entice long-form creators

The platform is using one-off payouts and premium placements to make up for its lack of the type of robust payout system offered by YouTube.

As Instagram makes its new push into long-form content via Features, the platform is also working on other ways to promote long-form video in order to get more people viewing and more creators posting.

A key question from creators, however, has been around monetization, and why they would want to post their longer videos to Instagram, given that its payout system is nowhere near as lucrative as YouTube’s Partner Program.

But even without a solidified payments system in place, Instagram does have some other tricks up its sleeve to get creators sharing more long-form videos.

As shown in these screenshots posted by creator economy expert Lia Haberman, Instagram is highlighting long-form video in a prominent space at the top of its Explore page for some users. That will certainly get the attention of creators, who will see this as a massive opportunity to get more people checking out their content.

Instagram is also reportedly offering one-off payments of up to $500,000 to a selection of big-name creators to get them posting long-form content.

In combination, it seems like Instagram will be able to make Features a bigger consideration for creators, even without a more competitive monetization program.

Though the platform is also working on that element.

Instagram Chief Adam Mosseri has acknowledged the app will have to evolve its payment program to incentivize long-form creators, but it hasn’t worked out the best way to move forward yet.

In an interview with YouTubers Colin and Samir published last week, Mosseri discussed the various challenges Instagram is facing in building a sustainable monetization program.

Mosseri said the aim of adding long-form content on IG was to compete with YouTube, which is now seeing most of its viewership growth via Connected TV, or people watching YouTube videos on their home TV sets. Mosseri said that Instagram, ideally, will be able to integrate short and long-form content into a more Instagram-style approach, where long-form videos can be split into short Reels, thereby providing viewers multiple consumption options.

But on payments, Mosseri said that Instagram was still weighing its options.

Mosseri added that he doesn’t believe platform payments, performance-based incentives or creator bonuses are a revenue driver. Inserting mid-roll ads can also be disruptive, and while Mosseri said Instagram is making one-off payments to bootstrap Features, that isn’t a sustainable path.

Which leaves few options, outside of helping creators organize brand deals to support their content. Mosseri said that Instagram offers certain smaller creator payment schemes, but those have also been historically cost-negative and a losing bet in terms of ROI.

Mosseri’s comments likely won’t give a lot of hope to creators looking to make more money via long-form content in the app. This is especially true given that an external analysis of Instagram’s payout program, conducted by VidIQ, shows that creators on Instagram generally only earn around 10% of what they make on YouTube.

That could eventually see Features going the same way as IGTV, the platform’s previous long-form content play. IGTV also died because there was no financial incentive for creators to post to Instagram instead of posting to YouTube.

Given Mosseri’s comments, it’s hard to see this outcome shifting , even with expanded exposure options.

#7260. Google Explains How Redirects Affect Ranking Signals via @sejournal, @martinibuster

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

Google explains how redirect choices affect ranking signals, canonicalization, and which URLs appear in Search.

Google’s Gary Illyes and Cherry Prommawin discussed how permanent and temporary redirects affect canonicalization and ranking signals. They explained that both can help Google recognize related URLs but that they also signal different intentions about which URL should represent the content in Search.

One of the first things they did was to set the table and explain the difference between a 301 and 302 redirect.

Google’s Cherry Prommawin started off the discussion by stating that regardless of what redirect is used, a web page is still going to be indexed by Google. She then affirmed that there are correct choices.

“First of all, relax. No matter which redirect you choose, one of your pages will stay in search and be part of AI training.

There are technically correct choices, but getting it wrong is not the end of the world. Google will still index a page. It’s really just a matter of which URL will show up.”

“Does Google actually care if both numbers, 301, 302, successfully take user to the new page anyway? Aren’t they basically doing the same job?”

“Well, yes and no. Google’s crawlers don’t care at all.”

The conversation next turns to deduplication which is the part where Google groups together similar pages then chooses one page as the one to represent all of the other similar pages in the search index.

“But we have to talk about the real battlefield here, the deduplication process.”

“Ah, the digital family reunion. This is where we look at a room full of identical siblings and try to organize them into a single happy family.

…So in search deduplication, our system have to group all pages with similar main content together. That’s clustering.”

Prommawin added that both 301 and 302 will have an effect on clustering similar pages together. But that only the 301 redirect will have a canonicalization effect.

…then our index has to select just one official URL to represent this entire flock in the search results. Basically, within a cluster, we choose one representative page.

The final selection process is called canonicalization.”

Illyes and Prommawin said that Google has a system for dealing with content that is very similar. Rather than rank no pages from a site, Google will choose to rank one page in a process called deduplication and the page that’s chosen to be representative of all the similar pages is called the canonical.

Some SEOs have this idea called content cannibalization. It’s a concept that began in the early 2000s to explain why pages weren’t getting indexed. The idea is that a group of pages are so similar that Google is choosing not to rank any of them because all of the ranking signals are diluted across all of the URLs with similar content.

But content cannibalization contradicts what we know as a fact about how Google treats similar content in the real world. Google clusters them and then chooses one as the canonical and transfers all of the ranking signals to that one URL.

What SEOs call “cannibalization” is really some other problem like a lack of uniqueness, thin content, and all the other real reasons that cause Google to not index a web page.

With a 301 redirect the ranking signals from the old page/pages will transfer to the new URL which will then be the canonical.

Gary explained how the 301 redirect works:

“Google takes all the ranking signals of the old page and hands them to the new page.”

Then he explained why the 302 does not transfer ranking signals:

“Google assumes you are going back eventually, keeps the old URL in the search results, and basically ignores the new URL.”

So if you use a 302 redirect then the new URL will be “basically” ignored by Google.

They next explained that 307 and 308 redirects serve the same kind of general temporary and permanent purposes as the 302 and 301 redirects. The difference is in how browsers handle submitted “post” data. Post is a reference to data that’s submitted from a web browser to a server using the HTTP POST method, such as when someone fills out a contact form and presses the “submit” button.

The only major difference is how they handle post data.

…Imagine our chicken is on your website on Black Friday and it just filled out a massive checkout form with its credit card and shipping details. It hits submit, but oh no, the site’s primary payment server is completely overloaded, so it triggers a quick failover redirect to the backup server.”

If a site uses a 302 redirect the data is lost. But if the site uses a 307 redirect the data is saved in the browser.

“But if you use a 307 redirect, it instructs the browser: Keep the data intact, keep the connection as a post request, and forward the exact same data to the backup payment processor.

The user just sees a brief loading wheel maybe, but the transaction goes through…”

Lastly, Google recommends using server-side redirects like 301s and 302s rather than the old “meta refresh” or JavaScript redirects.

“A server-side HTTP redirect is a clean, instant instruction. JavaScript and meta refreshes force the browser to download the page, parse the code, wait for the script to execute, and then launch the user somewhere else.”

The overall takeaway is that redirects are not just a way to forward visitors to another URL. They also communicate whether a move is permanent or temporary, influence Google’s canonicalization decisions, and can affect website functionality.

Google explained URL clustering and canonicalization, explaining that both 301 and 302 redirects work for clustering, but a permanent 301 is the recommended choice for identifying the canonical URL.

#7256. Facebook expands Creator Fast Track program to Brazil

The program offers incentives for creators not yet posting to Facebook, though the monetary incentives for Brazilian creators are significantly less than what the company offered in North America.

Meta wants to get more creators in Brazil posting to Facebook, so it’s expanding its Creator Fast Track program, which offers guaranteed monthly payments to entice creators who are already popular on other apps to share their content to Facebook as well.

Launched in the U.S. and Canada back in March, the Facebook Creator Fast Track program is aimed at creators who’ve already built an audience elsewhere.

To qualify for Brazil’s version of the program, local creators will need to have at least 20,000 followers on either Instagram, TikTok or YouTube, and have generated at least 30,000 video views over the previous 30 days.

The program is open to creators who haven’t posted a Facebook Reel in the last 6 months and is only valid for “high-quality, original” content.

Those accepted into the program will be required to post at least 20 original Reels on Facebook during each 30-day period over the course of three months. Those Reels need to be posted on separate days, and the videos must be original to Facebook. Creators can re-post content that they’ve shared in other apps, so long as it hasn’t been previously posted on their Facebook Page.

Creators who meet these requirements will be rewarded with guaranteed payments not tied to video performance, but instead linked to audience counts on other apps.

As explained by Meta: “Your monthly payment depends on the highest follower count on one eligible Instagram, TikTok or YouTube account linked during your application. The program uses only your highest follower count from a single account.”

Creators with up to 100,000 followers in one of these apps are eligible to receive $25 per 30-day period, and up to $75 total for the life of the program. Creators with one million followers or more on these other platforms will get $300 per day, up to $900.

Those amounts are significantly lower than what Meta offered creators in the U.S. and Canada in the first iteration of the program.

In the first iteration, creators in the U.S. and Canada with up 100,000 followers were offered up to $450 per month, while those with a million or more followers got $3,000 monthly payments.

It seems that Meta has watered down those amounts for the Brazilian market, which could suggest that the initial scheme didn’t deliver enough return on that outlay to justify the payouts.

Yet, at these lower amounts, it seems unlikely this will be a big lure for creators in Brazil, who, in most cases, are earning significantly more from monetization programs on other apps.

But clearly, Meta is keen to make Facebook more engaging, and by enticing established creators over, it’s hoping that it can then keep them posting to the app by showcasing the value of connecting with the Facebook audience, and tapping into increased video engagement in the app.

As part of its Q1 2026 earnings announcement, Meta reported that total video watch time on Facebook increased by more than 8% globally, representing the largest quarter-over-quarter engagement gain in four years.

#7255. GEO Isn’t A Distraction – Referral Data Misses Where AI Shapes Buying Decisions via @sejournal, @TaylorDanRW

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

This webinar uses Conductor's research to help you prioritize marketing data and AI visibility measurement fixes.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

9 AI Workflows For Better Content. Nov 18, 2 PM ET.

GEO's value goes beyond referrals: AI answers shape buying decisions before a click, and ChatGPT visitors convert at nearly five times the search rate.

In her examination of website referral numbers, Marie Haynes suggests that GEO represents a misplaced investment for digital teams.

By reviewing data across 145 million sessions (over 100 websites), the article demonstrates that visits from ChatGPT represent less than one percent of the volume delivered by traditional Google search. The sample size is strong, but as with anything in SEO and organic visibility, mileage varies.

I did my own follow-up study looking at 108 million sessions from Jan. 1 to Nov. 2, 2026 (over 57 websites), and came to a similar finding that total AI traffic amounted to 0.66% of the total. This means that for every 1,000 users from search engines, (trackable) AI sends seven.

This does vary a lot between websites, with some seeing 20-25 AI users per 1,000, but the numbers are still relatively low.

The findings also reveal that people arriving from ChatGPT convert at about five times the rate of typical searchers, yet the conclusion maintains that marketing teams should ignore directly looking at optimizing for AI because Google still provides the vast majority of total sales.

While the recorded traffic numbers are accurate, the strategic conclusion rests on assumptions that fail to match the real habits of people who use modern AI tools.

Judging the business value of conversational tools by counting immediate site visits applies an outdated measurement system to a new information environment, as users are behaving differently with AI interfaces than what they have done traditionally with a standard search engine.

Neglecting GEO as a principle because incoming sessions appear small ignores the fact that conversational answers shape purchasing choices long before any link receives a click, while it also overlooks the commercial value of visitors who convert at remarkable rates.

The foundation of the argument depends entirely on website analytics sessions, which register zero value whenever an answer engine satisfies an inquiry without sending a person to an external domain. This reporting method breaks down because conversational platforms exist to answer complex questions without requiring people to browse through lists of web pages.

As documented in coverage on zero-click search dynamics, automated summaries satisfy informational demand directly on the platform and reduce traditional outbound journeys. When a procurement team researches software packages or a shopper looks for reliable touring bicycles, the entire comparison takes place inside the conversation window.

A company buyer might spend half an hour discussing system security, pricing tiers, and practical trade-offs among several competing vendors. If an engine explains your product with precision and presents it as a sensible choice, that buyer needs no immediate link click because the required knowledge is already in hand.

Several days later, that same individual opens a standard web browser, searches for your brand name, and completes a demonstration booking. The analytics platform credits standard organic search with the resulting transaction, reinforcing the mistaken belief that Google generated the entire customer journey when the real choice occurred inside the chat interface.

Ignoring conversational optimization because direct referral traffic seems negligible resembles dropping brand sponsorship because people decline to click on road signs. Concentrating solely on immediate referral links creates an analytical blind spot, because the work of optimizing for generative engines focuses on brand placement within the data sources that modern engines trust.

Analyses into GEO principles highlight how modern engines prioritize clear entity relationships and structured knowledge over traditional ranking signals. If an engine ignores or misrepresents your company during this silent research phase, that eventual conversion via traditional search will never take place.

See also: The AI Search Metrics I Use To Track Client Pipelines

The research by Haynes contains a striking detail, showing that visitors arriving from ChatGPT converted at almost 14% while standard search visitors converted at 3%. Dismissing this huge performance gap simply because total volume remains modest values raw visitor numbers over genuine commercial results, which conflicts with sound marketing economics.

Standard search traffic contains substantial waste, including accidental clicks and casual browsing from people who possess no intention of purchasing anything. Pouring effort into capturing massive organic volume often produces diminishing returns, filling site logs with casual readers who quickly leave without taking action. In contrast, industry reports on B2B citation behavior confirm that buyers engaging with generative syntheses click-through primarily to confirm factual details and evaluate final purchase considerations.

A person who conducts an interactive evaluation inside a conversational engine and then follows an outbound reference link exhibits exceptional commercial intent, having already moved past basic research to understand product strengths and weaknesses.

For B2B suppliers, high-value consultants, and specialized retailers, aggregate visitor volume remains an empty vanity metric. A firm that gains several enterprise clients from a modest stream of qualified conversational referrals earns far more profit than one attracting thousands of random visitors who generate nothing.

Directing resources toward an audience that converts at such remarkable rates represents sensible commercial planning, so the right response to a 5x conversion advantage is to study the reasons behind that success and ensure that your organization appears whenever serious buyers seek recommendations.

See also: AI Mode Sends A Different Visitor. Your Website Wasn’t Built For Them

The structure of the internet continues to transform, and the analysis correctly notes that autonomous systems will alter future business activities across digital channels.

Turning away from GEO because referral numbers appear modest misunderstands where commercial decisions occur today. AI tools have reshaped how shoppers and corporate teams research and purchase products, and the influence of this behavioral change remains invisible on standard traffic dashboards even as it appears within the exceptional conversion rates of those who visit.

Treating generative engines as an unimportant diversion surrenders your presence at the point of decision, leaving the field open to competitors who recognize that influence matters far more than traditional clicks.

#7250. Snapchat posts modest user increase in Q3

Company revenue was up, but the platform’s daily active user growth has stalled across nearly every market.

Snap Inc. posted its Q3 2026 earnings report on Wednesday, which showed that user growth on Snapchat has largely stalled, while overall company revenue saw a steady year-over-year increase.

First, on users. Snapchat now has 495 million daily active users, up from 493 million in Q2.

An increase of just 2 million daily actives, quarter over quarter, is not a particularly positive endorsement of the app’s expanded market opportunities. But even more pressing, Snapchat saw no growth at all in daily active users in either the North American or European markets, meaning all user growth came from its Rest of World segment.

This could end up having a broader impact on the platform’s revenue potential, because Snapchat generates the majority of its money in North America and Europe.

Indeed, Snapchat’s revenue per user in North America was $11.07 in Q3, up from $10.26 the previous quarter. By contrast, the platform only generated $1.13 per user in the Rest of World. That means that Snapchat is only seeing user growth in regions where it earns 90% less per user, which is based primarily on ad intake.

It's possible that Snapchat could also be hit harder than other apps by expanded teen social media bans, which are being considered or implemented in multiple markets. According to Pew Research published last year, around 55% of U.S. teens between ages 13 and 17 use Snapchat, making it the fourth most popular platform among this cohort, after YouTube, TikTok and Instagram.

In Australia, which implemented social media usage restrictions for children under age 16 in December 2025, Snapchat said it subsequently locked or disabled more than 415,000 user accounts. These accounts, per Snapchat, had either been openly declared as belonging to a user under age 16, or Snapchat determined that was the case based on its age detection technology.

Snapchat said in August 2023 that it had more than 8 million users in Australia. That means the teen social media bans may have cost the platform around 5% of its total user base in that market.

Which may not seem like a major shift, but if Snapchat loses 5% of its users across the world, that would be significant, especially given its already shrinking growth margins. In addition, teen bans could also lead to Snapchat losing some of its value as the connection tool for younger audiences.

On the positive side, in his prepared remarks to accompny the earnings report, Snap CEO Evan Spiegel said that average daily messages sent in the app increased 8% year over year in Q3, while the number of people engaging in two-way communication increased 6% year over year for the same period.

Spiegel also noted that the number of people posting to Spotlight grew 130% year over year, while daily active viewers increased 40% year over year, underlining the enduring value of short-form video content.

On the revenue side, the company's intake rose 14% year-over-year to $1.72 billion.

By region, overall third-quarter revenue in North America was up 13% year over year to $1.01 billion, while Europe was up 22 % to $363 million, and the Rest of World was up 11% to $345 million. Again, the revenue discrepancy between regions is notable here. Snapchat has three times more users in the Rest of World market versus EU, so while the revenue numbers between them are close, that doesn't equate to the same value, while it still generates the vast majority of its intake from the U.S.

Even so, the fact that Snap is seemingly succeeding in deriving more revenue from its key markets, despite no user growth, is also worthy of note.

In the accompanying earnings release, Spiegel said that artificial intelligence has become a significant advantage for the company’s teams and its ad platform.

Snap has also been working to evolve its business offerings, with improved creator partnership opportunities. The company also started pitching its ads to B2B brands.

Snap’s other big opportunity, per Spiegel, is its AR Specs, which launched in September. The $2,200 device is now available for pre-order in the U.S., France and the U.K., though it isn’t being shipped to users just yet.

Snap seems to hope the glasses will bring about new opportunities in advanced digital connection via wearable digital overlays.

But initial data in Snap’s own Q3 report showed that Specs sales haven’t contributed meaningfully to its bottom line yet. In a statement, Snap CFO Doug Hott said that advertising revenue grew 4% and the company’s “Other Revenue grew 84%,” led by Snapchat+, Memories Storage, and Lens+.

Overall, Snap’s third-quarter earnings data showed that the company still has a long way to go in securing its future prospects, and without any insight on the potential market for its AR Specs, it’s impossible to gauge consumer interest or the value of this element.

Subscription growth is relevant, but Snap appears to have a lot of other factors working against it that may impact its future results.

#7247. ‘They hold us hostage’: Publishers furious over terms change still can’t quit Facebook

Join us Dec. 1-3 in Nashville for the Digiday Programmatic Marketing Summit

Publisher trade groups around the world are pressing Meta to change its planned terms of service, but heads of audience and social media told Digiday that their continued reliance on its platforms raises questions about how much leverage they have to force changes.

The tension is familiar: publishers want to stop tech platforms from using their journalism in AI tools without compensation, but can’t easily give up the reach those platforms provide.

Meta recently announced it would consult with industry groups and leaders before updating its terms, but that has done little to pacify publishers. Several publisher trade groups Digiday spoke with, including the News Media Alliance, Digital Content Next, and The Professional Publishers Association, said they hadn’t yet been in contact with them.

In a video posted to LinkedIn, Tami Hoffman, director of public policy at The Guardian, warned that some publishers may ultimately decide to abandon Meta’s platforms over the company’s updated terms of use.

“Policy moves slowly, and some publishers may take the tough decision to come off these platforms altogether. This issue is filling up my inbox,” Hoffman said in the video.

That possibility is already weighing on publishers as they watch to see if Meta makes changes to its terms or provides detail on how publishers can opt out, according to Melissa Chowning, founder and CEO of Twenty-First Digital, an audience development and marketing consultancy for publishers and brands.

“The ones paying close attention are still asking questions about whether Meta traffic is worth the trade-off. Now they just have more time to make that call. I suspect if the terms don’t change, we’ll see several publishers move off of Meta. Hard to say how many that would be,” she said.

But internally, the situation is more complicated.

And that’s because Meta’s value to publishers is not really its referral traffic anymore. Heads of audience at three publishers told Digiday Meta’s social platforms are still crucial for other audience development and business strategies: branded content, paid media, reach, engagement, awareness, and connecting with community. They aren’t ready to cut ties, putting into question how much leverage publishers really have in their fight against Meta’s AI scraping terms.

One head of audience said the publisher had deprioritized Facebook after traffic from Meta’s platforms fell from accounting for more than a fifth of its global traffic to a negligible share. But Instagram remains an important platform for brand awareness, community engagement and marketing rather than referral traffic, they said.

“Meta Zero is already here,” the head of audience said.

When analyzing social traffic to the top 10 publisher websites (including The New York Post, The Wall Street Journal, The New York Times, People, BBC, AP News, CNN, USA Today, Fox News and CNBC), Facebook traffic was down 5% year-over-year, according to Similarweb data shared with Digiday.

Meta’s platforms accounted for an average of 3.47% of global publisher traffic in July 2026, according to Chatbeat’s most recent data, which includes over 2,500 domains.

The head of audience said they didn’t think it was likely they would remove themselves from Meta’s platforms.

“They hold us hostage,” they added. “This change in terms is another brazen move by Meta.”

It echoes the debate over blocking Google’s crawlers. Publishers have threatened to do so over AI scraping, but have yet to follow through, partly because they still rely on Google for search discovery and traffic, even as referrals decline.

Publishers’ relationships with Google and Meta have some important parallels, but they also differ in significant ways, stressed Eilidh Wilson, head of policy and public affairs at the U.K. publisher trade body the PPA. “Both platforms wield enormous power over audience access, making withdrawal a ‘nuclear option’ for publishers,” she said.

The key difference is how publishers use these platforms, she explained. “Referral traffic from Meta has declined sharply, particularly since 2023, when it deprioritized branded content,” said Wilson. The result: publishers that had invested millions in social media teams and content saw traffic collapse almost overnight.

However, declining traffic has not diminished publishers’ reliance on Meta or its market power, stressed Wilson. “Publishers have adapted, using Meta for audience engagement, conversion and subscription acquisition,” she said. “A publisher may receive relatively little referral traffic from Meta but still depend on it for a significant proportion of new subscriptions. Referral traffic alone therefore understates Meta’s power over publishers. This is why we need an SMS [Strategic Market Status] investigation into Meta, as we’ve seen with Google Search.”

Another head of audience at a news publisher said social platforms remain critical to reaching new audiences, building awareness around its journalism and connecting with readers on platforms where they spend their time.

“I don’t see us abandoning our social accounts,” they said. “I think publishers can recognize the downsides of these relationships while still seeing tremendous value in being on these platforms. It’s not an either-or proposition.”

A third head of audience wasn’t yet certain how their outlet would evaluate its relationship with Meta going forward, as it was a decision that would be made at a more senior executive level. But they had strong words about the upcoming terms change.

“This is another example of Meta using brute force to get what it wants from the publishing industry. Publishers are being asked to accept sweeping access to their sites as the price of reaching audiences on Meta’s platforms. It’s a shameless pursuit of Meta’s own benefit, with little regard for the sustainability of the journalism its products depend on,” they said.

Meta has also recently started charging brands for link posts, which can help drive audiences to their sites. While publishers are excluded from this, the first head of audience wondered for how long.

All of this points to why a number of publishing trade bodies are turning to regulators and lawmakers to pressure Meta into changing its terms, leading to developments like the CMA investigation, the Norwegian court injunction and U.S. Senators urging Meta to abandon the proposed terms.

Wilson said Meta needs to “unbundle” its terms and separate access to its platform from permission to crawl publishers’ content. Publishers should be able to allow link previews or advertising while blocking uses such as AI training and product development, explained Wilson. Those choices need to be enforceable: protections such as robots.txt should be written into binding terms, not left to public assurances, she added.

Publishers should also be told which crawlers and third parties collect their content, what they collect and how that material is used. Meta’s terms, Wilson argues, should not retroactively authorize past copying or make publishers responsible for content they do not have the rights to license.

“This goes beyond communication and transparency. The terms themselves must change,” said Wilson. “As drafted, they leave publishers with an impossible choice: withdraw from services their businesses depend on, or accept crawling and use of their content without firm legal safeguards. Regardless of Meta’s intentions, that legal exposure is unacceptable.”

The New York Times, People Inc., News Corp and Reuters report revenue growth despite AI disruption, but face tougher comparisons ahead.

DoorDash’s announced plans to acquire Instacart for $17 billion point to the constant need to gain market share by diversifying services and offerings.

Political and news creators are relied on now more than ever, but what platforms are working for them? Which ones aren’t?

#7244. WPP Media posts first growth in 2 years as turnaround stays on track

Let Marketing Dive's free newsletter keep you informed, straight from your inbox.

Sequential quarterly improvements at WPP were cited by executives as evidence that Elevate28 is working, though creative further deteriorated in Q3.

WPP inched further toward a recovery in Q3, with like-for-like revenue less pass-through costs declining 2.1% to roughly $4.39 billion, or 3.27 billion pounds, according to an earnings statement. Organic revenue growth, a metric the ad-holding group shares for easier comparisons to its competitive set, was up 0.5% for the period.

Sequential quarterly improvements — LFL revenue was down 6.7% in Q1 and 2.8% in Q2 of this year — were cited by executives as signs that WPP’s Elevate28 turnaround plan remains on track, including the goal of returning to positive growth in 2027. The company upheld its full-year guidance for 2026.

The network’s media segment posted growth for the first time in two years and after seeing declines of 8.3% and 2.8% in Q1 2026 and Q2 2026, respectively. The U.S. was the biggest contributor to a 0.3% increase in revenue for media, WPP CEO Cindy Rose said on a call discussing the results with investors.

WPP Media secured large pieces of business that could provide future tailwinds. It retained global media duties for The Coca-Cola Company following a review that saw the North American division — which WPP previously held — switch from Publicis Groupe to Omnicom. WPP did not participate in the pitch for Coke’s North America account.

“We made a tough call between spending yet another six weeks pitching for North America versus delivering against what we had already won,” Rose said in response to an analyst question about the decision. “It was a fine judgment call, which I made, and it's as simple as that really.”

DoorDash deepened its relationship with WPP during the quarter, naming the agency network its first global media partner in September. DoorDash recently announced plans to acquire the grocery delivery platform Instacart for $17 billion, which could benefit WPP on the advertising front. WPP has worked with DoorDash for eight years but the remit now spans more than 40 markets.

Rose was asked about other agencies winning clients without engaging in a traditional pitch process. That is how Publicis nabbed PepsiCo from Omnicom, and Publicis has said it is generally picking up more business without needing to pitch.

“You're not going to find anybody that says, ‘I love pitching.’ The cost and burden of the process seems to be increasing. But I think what's really important to me is that my clients fully understand the implications of the decisions they're making,” Rose said on the call.

“These are really high-stakes decisions with material business consequences and they deserve careful consideration,” Rose added. “I don't think we do ourselves any favor as an industry of professionals by encouraging clients to bypass proper due diligence.”

WPP’s results were a mixed bag elsewhere. Its production arm grew 2.5% in Q3, building on positive gains from the prior quarter, but creative further deteriorated, down 5.1% year over year compared to a 3.5% decline in Q2. Some of the creative slide was attributed to polarization across different client verticals, with some industries pulling back on spending, as well as a slower ramp-up in new business.

WPP Creative is undergoing some leadership transitions as well. Jon Cook is departing VML after over three decades while Tyler Turnbull, formerly of Omnicom’s McCann, recently joined as global CEO of WPP Creative.
