#7176. Anthropic Strikes Its First Licensing Deal, With the AP, for Election Coverage

This story was originally published in On Background with Mark Stenberg, a free, weekly newsletter that explores the key themes shaping the media industry. You can sign up for it here.

If, by chance, you opened Claude on Election Night and asked it for an update on the latest polling data, you would have been treated to a novel sight: up-the-minute information, provided by and attributed to the Associated Press.

This new development was the result of a content and data licensing deal struck between the two organizations, which was struck just before the midterm elections, according to two people familiar with the matter.

Anthropic did not respond to a request for comment, and the AP did not provide a comment on the record. The financial specifics of the arrangement, as well as other critical details, such as its timeframe, were not disclosed by either party.

Nonetheless, the deal marks a dramatic evolution in strategy for Anthropic, as the artificial intelligence firm behind Claude had not, until Tuesday, struck a content licensing deal with any digital publisher.

In that regard, Claude differed dramatically from its primary competitor, ChatGPT, whose parent company OpenAI has negotiated more than a dozen content licensing deals with major publishers, including Axel Springer, News Corp., and The Atlantic, among others.

In fact, the reluctance by Anthropic to engage in any kind of content licensing discussion had become such a subject of intrigue within the industry that I explored the situation in August, in one of my more literally titled pieces.

At the time, digital media executives told me that the company genuinely believes that its scraping and use of publisher content is legally permissible under the concept of fair use. As such, it had not moved to enter into any such partnerships, nor had it indicated any plans to do so in the future.

So why change its tactics now, especially as the company prepares to go public in a matter of weeks?

FOR Scoops, Sharp Analysis, and Insider Context

The likely answer is that Anthropic finally admitted what publishers have long known to be true, which is that an information index without access to up-to-date data is dramatically limited in its utility.

Because Anthropic refused to strike content licensing deals with major news publishers, it lacked access to the kind of credible, timely reporting that consumers turn to in key moments like an election cycle.

Without the ability to intake data from premium publishers, whose websites block its crawlers, Anthropic could only cite what AI researcher Deana Burke calls the “unlocked web.” These low-credibility sites might serve as sufficient sources of information for low-stakes queries, but they cannot tell a user which politician won a Senate seat 10 minutes ago.

As such, Anthropic was facing down the prospect of its users finding its platform to be nearly useless as a source of real-time information on one of the most important days of the year for search queries.

Publishers had been blocking Anthropic, as well as other answer engines, to produce this very outcome. The tactic is effectively a digital embargo, with news outlets withholding their content in hopes of bringing answer engines to the bargaining table to strike licensing deals. And in this limited instance, the strategy worked.

Of course, the AP is a bit of an exceptional case: It generates the vast majority of its revenue via licensing and wire deals with other publishers, so much of its business is structured around striking these kinds of tie-ups already. The AP was also the first publisher with whom Google and OpenAI signed licensing agreements.

The AP also has the advantage of being a canonically non-partisan outlet—it positions itself as more of a service, like Getty Images for information, than a standard publisher, which often has an editorial slant one way or the other. For these reasons, the AP makes for an ideal partner for an AI firm looking to test out licensing opportunities.

This limited foray into licensing is by no means an indicator that Anthropic has irrevocably changed its licensing strategy. In all likelihood, the company will use the data from this experiment to determine whether it wants to go further down this path or not.

But the deal does make a compelling case for the value of credible, real-time information to answer engines. And if Anthropic wants to stake its claim as the dominant platform in the ecosystem, it is hard to imagine how it does so without premium publishers.

Closing on OpenWeb (SCOOP): The venture firm Savage Ventures is preparing a bid of between $60 million to $70 million to acquire OpenWeb, the bankrupt Israeli adtech firm once worth $1.5 billion, according to an inside source. A distressed sale would be the latest grim chapter in the long, sordid saga of OpenWeb, whose fortunes have fallen precipitously in recent years. The startup, whose 2021 Series E received investment from The New York Times, helps publishers monetize their comments sections, a commercial model whose appeal is dwindling as the open web continues to contract. Still, its data, publisher relationships, and on-page real estate make it a reasonable acquisition target at just 4% of its high watermark valuation.

Creator Agency Consolidation (EXCLUSIVE): On Thursday, the social-first agency LV8 merged with its sister company Mirage Digital. Following the merger, the newly combined firm raised a round of equity funding “in the high eight figures” from Velocity Capital, according to LV8 chief executive Griffin Haddrill. The deal is the latest in a series of creator and social-centric tie-ups in the agency space in recent weeks. In September alone, the social agency SAMY acquired the influencer shop Get Engaged, and Billion Dollar Boy bought the business-to-business influencer marketing agency Creator Authority. In the first half of 2026, there were 70 creator economy M&A transactions, up 23% year-over-year, according to Quartermast Advisors, and agencies have accounted for around a fifth of those. Expect more from me on this front in the next week or two!

People Inc. People: On Thursday, People Inc. laid off around 6% of its staff, a total of nearly 200 employees, according to an internal memo I obtained. The cuts primarily affected its print and “legacy digital” businesses, the latest in a growing constellation of data points that reflect the diminishing commercial prospects of the open web. Of course, People Inc. is not alone in its reductions: Netflix, Disney, and Skydance, among others, have all enacted layoffs ahead of the holiday season.

On Thursday, at the Creator Marketing Summit in New York, I spoke on-stage with Tyler Stewart, the head of marketing and creative partnerships at Gopuff. Before the panel, I asked Tyler about the news that had broken earlier that morning, that two of his competitors—Instacart and Doordash—planned to combine in a $17 billion merger.

He offered, understandably, a rehearsed response: that Gopuff is in a category of its own because it owns its inventory and delivers from its own locations, unlike the middleman services its peers provide. That point of differentiation is a cornerstone of the Gopuff business; but another, more salient difference is its heavy use of creator marketing and collaboration.

In June, Gopuff launched a coconut water brand with Tom Brady, and last month Druski took an equity stake in the company as part of a partnership. The tactics stem largely from fiscal responsibility—Tyler said several times that budgetary constraints were a factor in his decision-making—but it also positions the company to capitalize on the growing momentum behind the creator space.

Mark Stenberg: Creators are a cornerstone of Gopuff’s marketing. How did that come about, and how has the strategy evolved?

Tyler Stewart: We’re still a privately funded startup, and we do not have the budget, frankly, to spend crazy TV dollars. Back in the day, our approach was to scale, spray, and pray, but we’ve evolved. Now, we want to go deeper and longer with a core crew of partners who can help us punch above our weight and reach audiences we can’t reach ourselves.

Mark: You also build products with creators. How does that work?

Tyler: Because we’re first party, we have a full private-label business and manufacturing capabilities that we can bring to our partners, which allows us to have a little fun with it. But it’s also more complex. A lot of times, creators don’t know what they want to do or how to do it, so we need to be that partner for them.

Mark: What’s the best example of what this model can unlock?

Tyler: Two years ago for Halloween, we made a massive chocolate bar with Alix Earle. We delivered it to her house in L.A. for her party and generated content around it. Then we made a regular-size version for our whole network, and it was gone in three minutes.

Mark: How do you decide which creators to work with?

Tyler: There are massive celebrities we’ve onboarded thinking, “They have hundreds of millions of followers, they’re going to sell the shit out of this.” And then they don’t. So, can they get us eyeballs we wouldn’t get otherwise? Do they have a level of authenticity, where they actually give a shit about the product? What’s proven not to work for us is the transactional version.

Mark: Druski’s deal with Gopuff included equity. How do you think about ownership for creators?

Tyler: We don’t have cash, so we need somebody who believes in us and wants to ride this wave with us. Druski is hilarious, and we’re going to let him do what he does best. But he has to accept that he’s not getting the check that PrizePicks or T-Mobile would pay him for a commercial.

Mark: You can’t give equity to every creator. What does it take to earn it?

Tyler: Can you help us grow our brand and business? Not everybody can. We put a lot of performance deals in place: Help us get a certain number of customers, and I’ll give you more equity. Why would the business want you as an owner if you’re not helping the business?

#7175. Samsara: Account Development Representative II - Atlanta

Samsara (NYSE: IOT) is the pioneer of the Connected Operations™ Cloud, which is a platform that enables organizations that depend on physical operations to harness Internet of Things (IoT) data to develop actionable insights and improve their operations. At Samsara, we are helping improve the safety, efficiency and sustainability of the physical operations that power our global economy. Representing more than 40% of global GDP, these industries are the infrastructure of our planet, including agriculture, construction, field services, transportation, and manufacturing — and we are excited to help digitally transform their operations at scale.

Working at Samsara means you’ll help define the future of physical operations and be on a team that’s shaping an exciting array of product solutions, including Video-Based Safety, Vehicle Telematics, Apps and Driver Workflows, and Equipment Monitoring. As part of a recently public company, you’ll have the autonomy and support to make an impact as we build for the long term.

The Account Development Representative (ADR) job is the launching point for an individual looking for a successful career in SaaS sales. In this role, you will be at the front of Samsara’s go-to-market strategy. This includes hunting for leads, mapping enterprise accounts, and building demand for Samsara’s full line of products. This role is ideal for individuals looking to develop enterprise technology sales skills and to advance their careers. Successful ADRs earn accelerated commissions and head down the path for promotion into closing roles. You will work closely with top-tier executives and will see up close how sales organizations succeed. This is a hybrid position open to candidates residing in Atlanta, Georgia and the Phoenix Metro Area of Arizona, requiring 3 days per week onsite and 2 days remote.

The compensation range shown spans approved pay zones for this role. Your primary work location determines the range applicable to you, which may be narrower than the full range displayed.

Annual on-target earnings (OTE) range for full-time employees for this position is below and depends on your city of residence. Learn more about our total rewards and benefits below.

At Samsara, we build for the people who keep the global economy moving. We want owners, not passengers, which is why our rewards are designed to fuel high-impact builders. Our compensation program delivers above-market total compensation through a combination of base salary, performance-based bonus/variable pay, and equity (for eligible roles) in a high-growth public company. We meaningfully differentiate pay for our top performers, who have the opportunity to earn above-market compensation that can outpace the broader market over time.

Beyond compensation, we provide the foundations that enable long-term success: a flexible, employee-led remote model, a professional development stipend, comprehensive health and parental leave plans, and more. If you’re ready to build for the long term and own the outcome, your journey starts here.

At Samsara, we embrace a flexible working model that caters to the diverse needs of our teams. Our offices are open for those who prefer to work in-person and we also support remote work where it aligns with our operational requirements. For certain positions, being close to one of our offices or within a specific geographic area is important to facilitate collaboration, access to resources, or alignment with our service regions. In these cases, the job description will clearly indicate any working location requirements. Our goal is to ensure that all members of our team can contribute effectively, whether they are working on-site, in a hybrid model, or fully remotely. All offers of employment are contingent upon an individual’s ability to secure and maintain the legal right to work at the company and in the specified work location, if applicable.

At Samsara, we welcome everyone regardless of their background. All qualified applicants will receive consideration for employment without regard to race, color, religion, national origin, sex, gender, gender identity, sexual orientation, protected veteran status, disability, age, and other characteristics protected by law. We depend on the unique approaches of our team members to help us solve complex problems and want to ensure that Samsara is a place where people from all backgrounds can make an impact.

#7173. Data API Engineer at Supabase

We’re looking for a Data API Engineer to join our Data API Team and help improve the feature set, reliability and performance of our Data API, based on the PostgREST project.

This role is ideal for someone who thrives in async, fast-paced environments and is excited about building developer tools that scale to millions.

Work on PostgREST’s PostgreSQL driver (based on Hasql) and remove bottlenecks and improve it’s performance.

Work on PostgREST development of new features and bug fixes, aligned towards the goal of a multitenant Data API.

Resolve incidents and escalated support issues, while also improving observability and performance.

Collaborate cross-functionally with other teams to debug and solve integration issues with the Supabase platform.

Strong Haskell skills with at least 3 years of experience.

Solid knowledge of SQL and plpgsql scripting in PostgreSQL.

Strong track record of contributing to and engaging with open-source projects. Bonus points if you’ve maintained an open source project.

Strong experience with providing technical support to users with varying levels of technical expertise.

Experience with debugging applications under the Linux operating system.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

#7168. Software Engineer - Storage at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

Supabase Storage is a scalable and high performance object storage service and is one of our fastest growing services. The current landscape of tools for uploading, storing, processing and delivering assets is complicated. Developers are forced to think about regions, caching, codecs and security before they can start uploading anything.

We are looking for engineers to solve this problem once and for all!

You will be working across the entire stack - client libraries, CLI, CDN, the storage server and the deployment infrastructure. One day, you might focus on improving upload resilience in iOS applications; the next, you could be optimizing the S3 protocol for efficient retries or finding ways to accelerate upload processing.

You will be contributing to both our open source server and upstream dependencies we use, like the TUS server.

This role is ideal for someone who thrives in async, fast-paced environments and is excited about building developer tools that scale to millions.

Improving our analytics bucket offering and improving the storage of open file formats like iceberg and ducklake

Asset optimization to speed up delivery for websites and mobile applications

Building out our edge network to speed up uploads and build features like automatic cache invalidation

Improve the performance, observability, and reliability of our storage server

Work with the frontend team to build a fast, powerful storage explorer

Improve our APIs, client libraries and CLI to provide an exceptional end-to-end DX for our users

Resolve escalated support issues, while also identifying and resolving systemic issues across the platform

Have 5+ years backend engineering experience of running Node.js in production

Have experience with highly scalable, distributed systems

Are comfortable with working across the stack - backend application development, some infrastructure work including deploying, monitoring and scaling the service

Posses familiarity with observability tools (OpenTelemetry, Prometheus, Grafana) and performance tuning

Have experience with production-grade PostgreSQL usage, including tuning and troubleshooting

Demonstrate an ownership mentality: driving projects end-to-end

Display empathy towards developer experience

Prior experience working on developer tools

Solid knowledge of cloud infrastructure (AWS, GCP or Azure) and containerization (Docker, Kubernetes)

Direct experience designing and operating large file upload/download pipelines at scale

Deep, practical CDN integration and optimization knowledge

Experience with image and video processing tools (sharp, dssim, ffmpeg), formats and codecs (AVIF, WebM, Brotli) is a plus

Experience with the frontend ecosystem (Next.js, React, etc) is a plus

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

#7167. Why One Agency Traded Billable Hours for a Stake in a DTC Steak Brand

It’s not every day that a glittering gold briefcase, or “beefcase,” with a marbled 55-ounce Tomahawk steak winds up on one’s doorstep. #7162. Salesloft: People Operations Coordinator - Temp to FTE Opportunity

Job Title: People Operations Coordinator - Temp to FTE Opportunity, 3 month contract

This is a fully remote opportunity and can be worked from any location in the United States.

Salesloft is building the next era of enterprise revenue — one where teams make confident decisions powered by AI and real signals. By combining our scale, insights, and AI innovation, we're building the industry's first Predictive Revenue System, enabling humans and AI to work together to make smarter decisions and drive consistent growth.

With thousands of customers using our platforms every day, we have an unmatched view into how revenue is actually won — the Revenue Context that reveals what happens, when, and with what outcome. This gives us a unique opportunity to transform an entire category and set a new benchmark for how modern revenue teams operate.

Join us to help transform how companies around the world run revenue — and build the platform that will guide leading revenue teams into the future.

At Salesloft, our People Operations Coordinator is foundational to a high-performing People team. Each coordinator embeds with their assigned business function(s) (e.g., Engineering, Marketing, Sales) to provide full employee lifecycle support, from offer through exit. As the first point of contact for employees and managers, coordinators deliver high-volume, high-accuracy service across HR transactions, data, leave, employee relations support, and employee questions, creating a consistently excellent employee experience. They partner closely with People Business Partners, keeping the work moving, the data clean, and the PBP fully informed, and work alongside Total Rewards, Benefits and Mobility, and Talent Acquisition so programs run smoothly.

Each coordinator also serves as the expert for an assigned country, bringing local compliance knowledge, language, and time zone coverage to the team. Working within a shared services model, coordinators cover for one another across functions during peak periods and absences, and bring back improvements that strengthen the whole team. This is a broad, high-visibility role and a clear path toward People Business Partner or People Operations leadership.

About 60 - 70% of your time supports your assigned business function(s) and their People Business Partner. That includes lifecycle transactions, employee data, questions, ER support, and the people programs for your pod.

The remaining 30–40% supports the broader People team. You will help Total Rewards with compensation and benefits cycles, partner with People Systems on HiBob, data, and reporting, and support Talent Acquisition through the handoff from offer to onboarding. You’ll also serve as the expert for your assigned country and cover for teammates during peak periods.

The balance shifts with the business calendar. Expect more Total Rewards work during open enrollment and compensation cycles, for example. We review it regularly throughout the year.

This role begins as a three-month contract with the opportunity to convert to a full-time, permanent Salesloft position based on business needs and performance. The contract period gives you the chance to learn the team, the systems and the business function you support, and gives us the chance to work together before making a long-term commitment. We will check in with you along the way so you know how things are going.

We are looking for a service-minded, detail-obsessed People professional who takes pride in getting the work right the first time. You understand that every offer letter, record update, and employee question is someone’s experience of the company, and you treat each one that way. You are comfortable being the first point of contact for employees and managers, and you know when to resolve a request yourself and when to bring in a People Business Partner or specialist. You handle confidential information with discretion and communicate clearly in writing. You are equally at home working through a high volume of transactions and spotting the pattern behind them, and you bring ideas back that make the whole team better. You enjoy partnering with business leaders, adapt easily across time zones and cultures, and want to build toward a career as a People Business Partner or in People Operations leadership.

Our People Operations Coordinators, PBPs and specialists work as one team on a shared goal, and each role has clear ownership. Coordinators are the front door of the People organization: warm, responsive and exact, with the pride in getting it right that earns managers’ and employees’ trust. We are a global team across India, Mexico and the United States, we cover for one another, and we share what we learn so every pod gets better. We take on hard problems together, and we hold ourselves to a high bar for accountability, empathy and execution.Values: Move as One, Evidence over Ego, Lead from the Front, Make Your Mark, Customer-Inspired

#7159. Container Engineer at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth. More than 9 million developers and 250,000+ customers now build on Supabase, and the majority of new databases on the platform are created by AI app builders — so the systems underneath have to stay reliable while they grow fast.

Container Engineering (ConEng) owns the internal container platform that runs Supabase's multi-tenant, multi-region services — the managed Kubernetes platform that other engineering teams build and deploy on top of. We own cluster lifecycle and cluster classes, multi-cluster and multi-region topology, capacity, scheduling, isolation and runtime safety, the higher-level platform APIs, the Kubernetes deployment contract and Argo semantics, rollout primitives, and runtime configuration and secret injection.

Your job is to make the platform itself trustworthy, observable, and cheap to operate at scale — and to raise the reliability bar for everyone who depends on it. This is not a gatekeeper role. You'll make the reliable path the easy path: building the automation and platform APIs that let product teams run safely by default, instrumenting what we operate, and making sure that when something breaks we detect it and recover fast.

Tooling & ecosystem — deep knowledge of the container and orchestration landscape (Kubernetes, containerd/OCI, the CNCF ecosystem) and a clear point of view on how to compose it.

Software engineering — designing and building the platform's automation, controllers, and internal tooling as production code, including solutions that don't come pre-shaped by an existing tool.

Operational / SRE practice — the discipline (SLOs, error budgets, incident response, ORRs) that runs the platform at scale as a predictable one.

We expect you to be genuinely deep in at least one of these, and many of our strongest candidates are deep in two (usually leaning toward one). Bringing real strength across all three is rare and exceptional — and even then, no one is equally strong in all three at once, so you'd focus where you're most needed at any given time. Different profiles are welcome here; tell us which of these is your center of gravity.

Regardless of the skill set, all team members carry a share of the team's on-call rota, responding to platform incidents and driving them to resolution and follow-up.

Define SLIs/SLOs and error budgets for the container platform itself, and build error-budget policies that actually gate platform changes.

Own the Operational Readiness Review (ORR) process for new platform capabilities and major changes.

Design and track metrics for platform uptime, and increase observability into the platform by capturing the right metrics, logs, and traces across the platform layers.

Drive the incident-to-improvement pipeline: facilitate blameless postmortems, connect findings to readiness gaps, spot repeat failure patterns, and push through systemic fixes.

Build and maintain the platform's automation, controllers, operators, and internal tooling — writing production code and shipping it, not just wiring together existing tools.

Design and implement the platform-side CI/CD and rollout primitives that speed up platform deployments while working within proper change and release management.

Implement and maintain security automation for the platform: intrusion detection, automated remediation, and patch management.

Identify and quantify operational toil, and build the automation that removes it rather than just documenting it.

Contribute to the platform's continual testing automation — the test suites and pipelines that validate platform releases are fit for purpose before they reach tenants.

Act as a platform reliability and architecture expert — contribute to failure-mode analysis, dependency mapping, and resilience design for multi-tenant container workloads.

Evaluate and evolve the platform's tooling ecosystem (orchestration, runtime, networking, isolation, and observability stack) with a clear, opinionated view on trade-offs.

Improve system architecture to reduce infrastructure cost while balancing security, reliability, and performance across tenants.

Guide teams building on the platform to design sustainable on-call practices for their own services: alert quality, escalation paths, runbook coverage, and noise reduction.

Have 5+ years in platform engineering, container/Kubernetes platform operations, SRE, or production infrastructure, and have operated production systems at scale and carried on-call for them.

Are genuinely deep in at least one — ideally two — of the three strength areas below (and tell us which is your center of gravity):

Tooling & ecosystem — know the container/orchestration landscape well: Kubernetes, containerd and the OCI/CRI standards, and the surrounding CNCF ecosystem (e.g. Argo CD/Argo Rollouts for GitOps and progressive delivery; Cilium/eBPF networking and the Gateway API; Karpenter/KEDA-style autoscaling; and workload-isolation runtimes such as gVisor or Kata Containers). You reason about trade-offs rather than reaching for defaults.

Software engineering — write real production code (Go and/or a comparable systems language) and can build controllers, operators, and platform APIs — stepping back to the underlying problem and building the right solution when no off-the-shelf tool fits.

Operational / SRE practice — are fluent in SLOs, error budgets, and operational KPIs; have led incident response and blameless postmortems; and have driven down MTTD/MTTR for systems you operated.

Have worked with large-scale multi-tenant systems and understand isolation as a spectrum — from namespaces, RBAC, network policies, and resource quotas through to stronger primitives such as virtual clusters (e.g. vCluster) or VM-class runtimes.

Operate confidently on AWS (multiple accounts, IAM, VPC) and are comfortable with infrastructure-as-code (Pulumi preferred; Terraform/CDK also fine) and Kubernetes.

Script and automate to eliminate toil rather than absorb it.

Communicate clearly with both infrastructure specialists and product engineers, and thrive in async, globally distributed teams.

Are comfortable navigating ambiguity and iterating toward better systems over time.

Experience building and operating a multi-tenant Kubernetes platform that internal teams deploy onto.

Familiarity with policy engines (Kyverno, or OPA/Gatekeeper) and cost-attribution tooling (OpenCost/Kubecost) for per-tenant chargeback/showback.

Experience with managed database platforms or Postgres.

Familiarity with OpenTelemetry, Prometheus/VictoriaMetrics, Grafana, or similar observability tooling.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

#7155. Gallery: Inside Gold Diwali, a Historic Celebration by Gold House

The lights were bright and so were the stars at Gold Diwali.

The Oct. 29 event in Los Angeles was the first-ever celebration of the Festival of Lights by Gold House, the Asian Pacific cultural institution providing opportunity and supporting the work of its community.

The organization’s new tentpole event, held at The Majestic Downtown, honored the heritage, achievements, and contributions of the South Asian diaspora around the world.

Attendees spanned prominent voices including CEOs, creators, and community members, including the acting and production team behind Ramayana: Rise of a Legend, which is set to be the most expensive Indian film ever made at $500 million and will debut globally on Nov. 5.

Leaders spanning entertainment, media, fashion, and business co-hosted the evening, including actors Freida Pinto, Dhar Mann, and Priyanka Kedia, as well as fashion designer Tan France, Blumhouse president Abhijay Prakash, and Poshmark founder Manish Chandra, Anita Chatterjee.

“Diwali is a celebration of light, community, and connection, and we wanted to bring that spirit to our Gold Diwali while honoring how culture shapes who we are and the possibilities we create,” said Bing Chen, executive chairman and CEO of Gold House.

The organization also announced a new partnership with nonprofit film fund South Stack Studios, called the Gold Stack Shorts Fund, to support short films and microseries focusing on South Asian stories. Applications are being accepted through Nov. 30.

Gold Diwali guests participated in traditional Diwali customs, with a South Asian menu by Rasoi Catering and music from DJ MissNinja. Entertainment also included a live tabla performance by KVS Tabla, a jewelry experience by Bhindi Jewelers, and a portrait studio with dating app Dil Mil and The Lounge Booth. Additional sponsors included Smartwater, Glance, McKinsey & Company, and Google’s 100 Zeros. The event was produced by BMF Productions.

More than a thousand multinational employers, health plans, and health systems trust Carrot to support millions of members across 195 countries – from pre-pregnancy through menopause and major life moments in between. Carrot's comprehensive clinical program delivers industry-leading cost savings for plan sponsors and award-winning experiences and improved outcomes for millions of people worldwide.Carrot is widely regarded as a defining force in healthcare innovation as a recipient of several top-tier awards, including Fast Company's 'Most Innovative Companies' and CNBC's '100 Barrier Breaking Startups'. Learn more at get-carrot.com.

Carrot is expanding its telemedicine clinic with a network of part-time, fully remote physician consultants who deliver evidence-based virtual care to members across multiple U.S. states. Depending on your licensure, certification, and area of expertise, you will provide consultations in menopause and perimenopause care aligned to The Menopause Society and ACOG guidelines, early fertility support aligned to ACOG and ASRM guidelines, or both.

Metabolic health is a core, cross-cutting component of this program. Weight, insulin resistance, and cardiometabolic risk are central to outcomes across every journey we support, from ovulatory function and preconception readiness to menopausal symptom burden and long-term cardiovascular and bone health. You should expect metabolic assessment and management to be a routine part of your visits, and clinicians comfortable prescribing and managing anti-obesity medications, including GLP-1 receptor agonists, within clinical protocols are especially encouraged to apply.

The clinicians in this network are the face of that experience, and the quality of their judgment, communication, and documentation determines whether members receive care they trust.

This role also anchors Carrot's clinical point of view that metabolic health is inseparable from reproductive and hormonal health. As the telemedicine program scales across states and journeys, the clinicians who join this network will directly expand access to guideline-aligned, whole-person care for members who often cannot find it locally.

#7147. Amazon’s ‘Joy Delivered’ Holiday Ad Swaps Product Showcase for a Heartfelt Homecoming

Amazon’s 2026 holiday campaign riffs on an experience most can relate to: the Odyssean effort of making it back home to loved ones in time, battling inclement weather and unreliable public transport.

Luckily for the lead character—Joy, played by New Zealand actress Kate Laidler, who finds herself stranded at an airport after all flights are canceled—the kindness of strangers prevails.

The two-minute thirty-second spot, “Joy Delivered,” follows her journey as she hits a dead end with her bus, hitchhikes with donkey farmers, spends a night in a cozy boat cabin on the high seas, and hails a cab from a giant to make it home just in time. As thanks along the way, Joy hands out the gifts originally intended for her family.

“The holidays are one of the busiest times of year, and Amazon’s role is to make the season easier so customers can spend more time on what matters most,” Jo Shoesmith, Amazon’s vp and global chief brand officer, told ADWEEK. “Whether that’s helping them find thoughtful gifts across a vast selection or making sure those gifts arrive quickly through Prime, Amazon takes care of the details so customers can focus on the people they love.”

The campaign aims to strike a more emotional tone than last year’s holiday ad, “Every Thing for Every Holiday,” which highlighted the breadth of what Amazon offers, said Shoesmith.

“The biggest challenge was getting the tone right,” Shoesmith said, noting the spot’s humor and whimsy, but that the emotion had to feel “earned and real.”

It’s a tough balance to strike. Removing gift-giving entirely from holiday ads isn’t easy, and it can become a lightning rod. The John Lewis holiday campaign this year has faced some backlash for its portrayal of “spoilt” grandchildren.

“The other challenge with Christmas advertising is finding the balance between familiar and surprising,” Shoesmith said. “People love the nostalgia of the season, but they also want to see something they haven’t seen before.”

The spot is scored by a bespoke rendition of The Beatles’ “Golden Slumbers” and will run across owned and social channels, as well as paid media, TV, CTV, and cinema.

The campaign was created by Amazon’s in-house creative team Amazon XCM. It also worked with video production company Smuggler, production company Work Offline, post-production studio Alt.vfx, and media agency Omnicom NYC, plus Amazon’s internal social team MODE.

Amazon reported its earnings last week, posting its highest-ever operating margin in Q3 of 13.9%.

Lucinda Southern is Adweek's managing editor of media and technology.

Select ADWEEK as one of your favorite sources and stay up to date on our latest content.

#7146. Carrot: Telemedicine Registered Nurse (RN), Consultant

Headquarters: Remote

About Carrot:

Carrot is the leading global fertility and family care platform, built on intelligent care orchestration: the right clinical guidance, at the right moment, in the context of each member’s life. More than a thousand multinational employers, health plans, and health systems trust Carrot to support millions of members across 195 countries – from pre-pregnancy through menopause and major life moments in between. Carrot's comprehensive clinical program delivers industry-leading cost savings for plan sponsors and award-winning experiences and improved outcomes for millions of people worldwide.

Carrot is widely regarded as a defining force in healthcare innovation as a recipient of several top-tier awards, including Fast Company's 'Most Innovative Companies' and CNBC's '100 Barrier Breaking Startups'. The company is regularly cited by leading global outlets — including The Economist, Bloomberg, The Wall Street Journal, NPR, ABC News, and Harvard Business Review — as a leading voice on digital health, the future of work, and family health. Learn more at get-carrot.com.

The Opportunity

Carrot is expanding its telemedicine clinic with a network of part-time to full-time, fully remote Registered Nurses who deliver evidence-based virtual care to members across all U.S. states. As an RN in this network, you are often the first clinical touchpoint for members navigating fertility, menopause, hormonal, and metabolic health journeys, providing triage, education, care coordination, and documentation that shape the entire care experience.

All work in this role is performed within RN scope of practice. RNs provide triage, education, care coordination, and documentation; they do not diagnose, do not prescribe, and do not independently modify a treatment plan. Anything requiring clinical judgment beyond RN scope is routed to a physician, so sound escalation judgment is central to the role.

This is a contractor engagement of approximately 20-40 hours per week, with scheduled shifts within the Monday-Friday, 8:00a-6:00p CST coverage window. 

What You’ll Own

In this role, you will:

  • Serve as the first clinical reviewer of inbound member messages, triaging for urgency, escalating to a physician when needed, and routing non-clinical threads to the appropriate support team.
  • Deliver member education on menopause staging, hormone therapy, preconception health, metabolic health, and medication administration.
  • Review and triage incoming lab results, escalate critical findings per protocol, retrieve outside records, and coordinate specialist referrals.
  • Manage pharmacy callbacks and prior authorizations in coordination with the care team.
  • Document all clinically relevant interactions same-day, create tracked internal tasks with named owners, and follow established escalation pathways.
  • Maintain active NLC multi-state licensure, complete required credentialing and onboarding, and adhere to HIPAA, continuing education, and liability coverage requirements.
  • Contribute to chart audits and clinical protocol feedback as the telemedicine program scales.

What Success Looks Like  

In your first 12–18 months, you will have:

  • Established a consistent record of accurate, protocol-aligned triage with appropriate physician escalation and no missed critical lab values.
  • Maintained same-day documentation and timely lab follow-up, with no outstanding credentialing or compliance gaps.
  • Delivered high-quality member education across menopause, preconception, hormone therapy, and metabolic health topics, reflected in strong member experience results.
  • Managed care coordination workflows reliably within your coverage window, including referrals, records retrieval, prior authorizations, and pharmacy callbacks.
  • Contributed to chart audits, protocol feedback, and quality improvement as the telemedicine program scales.

About You

You likely have:

  • An active, unrestricted RN license with NLC (Nurse Licensure Compact) multi-state privileges, in good standing, plus an ADN or BSN from an accredited program (BSN preferred).
  • A minimum of 3 years of active RN clinical experience, with meaningful experience in women's health, reproductive health, OB/GYN, fertility, endocrinology, Urology, Mens Health, or menopause care.
  • Demonstrated nurse triage experience, including assessing urgency, applying protocol, escalating appropriately, and recognizing critical lab values.
  • A clear understanding of RN scope of practice and sound judgment about when to escalate.
  • Strong written communication for asynchronous messaging and a strong phone presence for symptom assessment and instruction.
  • Proficiency with EHR and telehealth systems, secure messaging, and e-fax workflows.
  • Familiarity with HIPAA and applicable telemedicine privacy, security, and regulatory requirements.
  • The ability to commit to scheduled shifts within the Monday-Friday, 8:00a-6:00p CST coverage window, with a HIPAA-compliant, private home workspace and reliable internet.

Nice to Have ⭐

While not required, we would be especially excited if you bring:

  • Experience in a dedicated menopause clinic, fertility center, REI practice, or comparable specialty program.
  • Familiarity with preconception and fertility workup, including AMH and hormone panels, ovulation tracking, and cycle-monitoring labs.
  • Hormone therapy education experience, including prior authorization workflows.
  • Experience supporting members on anti-obesity medications, including GLP-1 receptor agonists.
  • Prior experience in digital health, health benefits, or multi-state telemedicine environments.
  • Experience contributing to clinical protocol development, quality improvement, or member-facing clinical content.
  • Experience supporting diverse and historically underserved populations, including LGBTQ+ and gender-diverse individuals.
  • Relevant certification (e.g., MSCP through The Menopause Society, RNC-OB) and/or fluency in an additional language.

 

Why This Role Matters

Carrot's RN network is the front line of the telemedicine clinic. RNs are typically the first clinician a member reaches, and the quality of their triage judgment, education, and follow-through directly determines member safety, trust, and the experience of care during some of the most personal moments of members' lives.

This role also keeps the clinical program running well as it scales. Reliable triage, same-day documentation, and disciplined escalation protect physician capacity for the decisions that require it, while expanding access to guideline-aligned fertility, menopause, and metabolic health support for members who often cannot find it locally.

Why Candidates Join Carrot

Carrot offers the opportunity to do meaningful work in service of a deeply human mission.

Highlights include:

  • A mission-driven company focused on expanding access to fertility and family-forming care globally.
  • A high-growth environment where leaders are empowered to shape strategy and drive impact.
  • Competitive compensation, equity, and comprehensive benefits.
  • Flexible Time Off and a culture that actively encourages employees to recharge.
  • Comprehensive parental leave and family-forming benefits.
  • A collaborative, inclusive, and values-driven culture.
  • The opportunity to work alongside passionate, thoughtful teammates committed to improving healthcare outcomes worldwide.

Compensation: 

Carrot offers a holistic Total Rewards package designed to support our employees in all aspects of their life inside and outside of work, including health and wellness benefits, retirement savings plans, short- and long-term incentives, parental leave, family-forming assistance, and a competitive compensation package. The starting base salary for this position will range from $40-$50/ hr. Actual compensation may vary from posted base salary depending on your confirmed job-related skills and experience. 

 

Fraud and Security Notice: Please note that all communication regarding job opportunities at Carrot will come exclusively from an @get-carrot.com email address. If you receive messages from any other domain, please disregard them and report the incident to: securityreporting@get-carrot.com

 

A Note on AI

At Carrot, we believe AI can help us work smarter, move faster, and create more meaningful experiences for our members, customers, and each other. While not every role requires deep technical AI expertise, we value people who are curious, eager to learn, and excited to thoughtfully incorporate AI into their day-to-day work.

For some roles, AI fluency is a core requirement—and we'll call that out clearly in the qualifications. For everyone else, we're looking for people who embrace new ways of working, use AI responsibly to improve quality and efficiency, and continue to learn as the technology evolves.

Why Carrot?

Carrot has received national and international recognition for its pioneering work, including Fast Company's Most Innovative Companies and World Changing Ideas, Inc. Power Partners, and Modern Healthcare’s Innovators. Carrot’s global workforce has been acknowledged with several accolades, including Fortune’s Best Workplaces in Healthcare, Great Place to Work, and Age-Friendly Employer certifications. Carrot is regularly featured in media reporting on issues related to the future of work, women in leadership, and healthcare innovation, including MSNBC, The Economist, Bloomberg, The Wall Street Journal, CNBC, National Public Radio, Harvard Business Review, and more. #7144. Chief Customer Officer at Xapo Bank

Diversity is at the heart of who we are at Xapo Bank. We’re a fully distributed team of over 160 Xapiens that work remotely from 50+ countries around the world.

Our beginning: A world that enjoys economic freedom and wealth protection,no matter where you live or who is running your country.To achieve that, we search the world for the best people for the job. We work hard, think globally, and inspire each other to learn and grow. We are committed to changing the way things are done.

Although we are headquartered in Gibraltar, this is a full time, 100% remote position Work from anywhere!

Xapo's product suite has grown from a Bitcoin vault into a full member proposition: BTC and USD accounts that pay interest, a global card with Bitcoin cashback, Bitcoin-backed loans, a growing suite of BTC wealth and credit products, and access to stocks, ETFs, and stablecoins, all inside one regulated relationship.

Xapo is a bank, and a bank earns on the money its members choose to hold with it. Each of those products shipped well. What hasn't yet existed is a single owner of the economics of the customer relationship: one person accountable for winning the right members, growing what they hold with us, and keeping them, and for the balance-sheet products that make that possible.

The Chief Customer Officer is a new role at the top of the house, reporting to the CEO. You will run the stream that grows what members hold with Xapo, and you will own the Customer P&L, retail and institutional. It brings growth, client coverage, product management, and product design under one leader who is judged on customer contribution, the assets attracted and kept, and the members who stay. This is a customer-focused role in the most commercial sense: what a member earns from us, not the feature we shipped, is the centre of every decision.

The measure of success is a Customer P&L that grows on what members hold and keep: LTV:CAC improving, approved-to-paid rising, churn falling, and customer contribution the business can plan against.

This is a banking role, not a requirements-gathering role, a marketing role, or a role you can run from a dashboard. Products are priced on the balance sheet and judged on the money they attract and keep.

This part matters as much as the mandate. The known failure mode for this role, in any company, is a CCO with a polished customer-insight deck and no ownership of the number. That is disqualifying here.

Day 30: The Customer P&L baseline is documented across retail and institutional: customer contribution, LTV:CAC, approved-to-paid, churn, net paid-member growth, and assets attracted and kept. You have formed your own independent view of where members are won, lost, and under-served, and shared it with the CEO and Board.Day 60: The unified Customer operating model is live: one set of key results across Growth, Client Coverage, Mission PMs, and Product Design, read weekly from one source. Every mission PM owns a product's contribution and its price, and the roadmap is ranked on what it earns, built with Finance and the line leads.Day 90: At least one measurable improvement in LTV:CAC, approved-to-paid, churn, or net new AUM flow is live and attributable to a change you made, not a feature ship, an outcome moved. Every cell in your line has defined key results and an owner who explains the number every week.

Shape the FutureImprove lives through cutting-edge technology, work 100% remotely from anywhere in the world.

Great work-life balanceBuild amazing things with a balance of autonomy and collaborative teamwork. Set your own work schedule and make use of a flexible PTO plan when you need to recharge.

Expect ExcellenceCollaborate, learn, and grow with a high-performance team. Learn how you learn best - from books to conferences, you’ll get a yearly budget for your individual learning and development goals.

At Xapo, we prioritize consumer protection and adhere to regulatory requirements by ensuring that all Xapiens are accountable for upholding principles of fair treatment, transparency, and ethical conduct in their interactions with customers and stakeholders.

#7107. Customer Growth Manager at Uscreen

They stall because they priced wrong, never built an email list, or launched an offer nobody wanted. Coaching is how we fix that, and it's part of the product rather than an add-on.

You own a segment, and you run it like a growth marketer.

We have a cohort of smaller video businesses where retention is not where it should be. Nobody owns it today. You will. The revenue that stays in that segment, and the revenue that grows inside it, is yours.

This is a coaching role for someone with a growth marketer's mindset. We wouldn't hire a demand gen marketer who couldn't analyze the state of the business and figure out what's needed, and we won't hire a coach who can't either. You'll look at a customer's business the way an owner would: where the revenue comes from, where it leaks, and what to do next. You pull your own numbers to get there. When a customer needs a custom report to answer a growth question, you build it yourself.

It's a one-to-many job. Your cohort is larger than a 1:1 book, so the work is designing coaching that scales: cohort programs, group sessions, office hours, and triggered outreach when the data says a business is drifting. You'll build that motion rather than inherit it.

Success is measured in dollars: gross revenue retention in your cohort first, expansion second. Not tickets closed, not calls booked.

A coach with a growth marketer's mindset. Maybe you've run growth or demand gen for a small business, built your own audience, or coached founders through their numbers. You understand how a membership business makes money, you pull your own analytics to see what's working, and you're comfortable doing it in front of a client. What matters is that you can find the real constraint in a business, then sit with the operator and say the honest thing.
